### Tracing and Reversing Rank-One Model Edits
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20819v1)
- **Authors**: Paul Youssef, Zhixue Zhao, Christin Seifert, Jörg Schlötterer
- **Abstract**: Knowledge editing methods (KEs) are a cost-effective way to update the factual content of large language models (LLMs), but they pose a dual-use risk. While KEs are beneficial for updating outdated or incorrect information, they can be exploited maliciously to implant misinformation or bias. In order to defend against these types of malicious manipulation, we need robust techniques that can reliably detect, interpret, and mitigate adversarial edits. This work investigates the traceability and reversibility of knowledge edits, focusing on the widely used Rank-One Model Editing (ROME) method. We first show that ROME introduces distinctive distributional patterns in the edited weight matrices, which can serve as effective signals for locating the edited weights. Second, we show that these altered weights can reliably be used to predict the edited factual relation, enabling partial reconstruction of the modified fact. Building on this, we propose a method to infer the edited object entity directly from the modified weights, without access to the editing prompt, achieving over 95% accuracy. Finally, we demonstrate that ROME edits can be reversed, recovering the model's original outputs with $\geq$ 80% accuracy. Our findings highlight the feasibility of detecting, tracing, and reversing edits based on the edited weights, offering a robust framework for safeguarding LLMs against adversarial manipulations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Tracing and Reversing Rank-One Model Edits":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the traceability and reversibility of knowledge edits performed on Large Language Models (LLMs) using the Rank-One Model Editing (ROME) method. The authors demonstrate that ROME introduces distinctive distributional patterns in the edited weight matrices. These patterns can be used to: 1) locate the edited weights, 2) predict the factual relation that was edited, 3) infer the edited object entity directly from the modified weights, and 4) reverse the edits, recovering the model's original outputs.  The study highlights the feasibility of detecting, tracing, and reversing edits based on edited weights, providing a framework for safeguarding LLMs against malicious manipulations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects regarding the security of LLMs. Previous work often focused on detecting *whether* a model has been edited by examining hidden states or output probabilities, and typically required a set of potentially edited facts to examine. This work takes a more proactive stance by investigating *how* ROME edits modify the model's weights and leveraging those modifications for detection, relation prediction, object inference, and edit reversal, without relying on prior knowledge of the edited facts. The approach to infer edited objects directly from the weight changes, rather than relying on prompt analysis, is a significant advancement. The exploration of bottom-rank approximations for reversing edits is also a fresh approach.

**Significance:** The malicious manipulation of LLMs through knowledge editing presents a real and growing threat. By providing techniques to detect, trace, and reverse these edits, this paper makes a crucial contribution to the field of LLM security and trustworthiness. The ability to detect edits based on weight changes alone is powerful as it doesn't require monitoring specific factual queries. The ability to infer the manipulated object is a significant step towards identifying the misinformation that was injected. The demonstration of edit reversal opens up possibilities for mitigating the effects of malicious modifications. The techniques developed in this paper have direct implications for ensuring the integrity and reliability of LLMs in real-world applications.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it easy to follow the methodology and understand the results.
*   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis of the effects of ROME edits on model weights.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents strong experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed techniques. The accuracy of relation prediction and object inference is impressive. The edit reversal results, while not perfect, still represent a substantial step forward.
*   **Practical Implications:** The proposed techniques have practical implications for safeguarding LLMs against malicious manipulations.
*   **Addresses an Important Problem:** It tackles a significant real-world issue around the potential manipulation of LLMs, and offers a robust set of possible mitigations

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Focus on ROME:** The paper's focus on ROME limits the generalizability of the findings to other knowledge editing methods. While ROME is a popular technique, it is essential to investigate whether the proposed techniques can be adapted to other editing approaches.  A discussion of the limitations regarding different KE methods would add to the robustness of the paper.
*   **Limited Dataset Diversity in the Main Paper:** The primary experiments use the CounterFact dataset. While an additional dataset is used in the appendix, the main results would be stronger with results shown on datasets with greater diversity.
*   **LLAMA3 Results Weaker:** The results obtained on LLAMA3 are often weaker than those on GPT2-XL and GPT-J. While this is acknowledged and partially explained, it raises questions about the effectiveness of the proposed techniques on more recent LLMs.  Further investigation may be required to address this performance disparity.
*   **Limited Discussion of Computational Cost:** While the paper highlights the feasibility of the techniques, there is limited discussion of the computational cost associated with detecting, tracing, and reversing edits. An analysis of the runtime complexity of the different steps would be valuable. The practical aspects of edit reversal can also be further detailed - how long does it take and what level of expertise is needed.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents novel and significant contributions to the field of LLM security. The techniques for detecting, tracing, and reversing knowledge edits have practical implications for safeguarding LLMs against malicious manipulations. While the focus on ROME and limited dataset diversity are weaknesses, the strengths of the paper outweigh these limitations.  The ability to identify and reverse edits *without* needing a specific hypothesis about the manipulated fact is a significant step forward.

### MT-Mol:Multi Agent System with Tool-based Reasoning for Molecular Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20820v1)
- **Authors**: Hyomin Kim, Yunhui Jang, Sungsoo Ahn
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have large potential for molecular optimization, as they can gather external chemistry tools and enable collaborative interactions to iteratively refine molecular candidates. However, this potential remains underexplored, particularly in the context of structured reasoning, interpretability, and comprehensive tool-grounded molecular optimization. To address this gap, we introduce MT-Mol, a multi-agent framework for molecular optimization that leverages tool-guided reasoning and role-specialized LLM agents. Our system incorporates comprehensive RDKit tools, categorized into five distinct domains: structural descriptors, electronic and topological features, fragment-based functional groups, molecular representations, and miscellaneous chemical properties. Each category is managed by an expert analyst agent, responsible for extracting task-relevant tools and enabling interpretable, chemically grounded feedback. MT-Mol produces molecules with tool-aligned and stepwise reasoning through the interaction between the analyst agents, a molecule-generating scientist, a reasoning-output verifier, and a reviewer agent. As a result, we show that our framework shows the state-of-the-art performance of the PMO-1K benchmark on 17 out of 23 tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MT-MOL, a multi-agent framework for molecular optimization using Large Language Models (LLMs). It aims to improve upon existing LLM-based approaches by incorporating structured reasoning, interpretability, and comprehensive tool-grounded processes. MT-MOL decomposes the molecular optimization process into four specialized roles: analyst, scientist, verifier, and reviewer, each handled by a dedicated LLM agent. The system integrates a comprehensive set of RDKit tools categorized into distinct domains (structural descriptors, electronic properties, etc.), managed by expert analyst agents to provide chemically grounded feedback.  The interactions between these agents enable tool-aligned and stepwise reasoning, ultimately generating optimized molecules. The paper evaluates MT-MOL on the PMO-1K benchmark, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on a significant portion of the tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the **architectural design** of the multi-agent system specifically tailored for molecular optimization. The decomposition into distinct roles (analyst, scientist, verifier, reviewer), the explicit integration of RDKit tools managed by expert analyst agents, and the emphasis on structured reasoning and iterative feedback loops are well-defined features that contribute to originality. While individual components (LLMs, RDKit) are not novel, their integration in MT-MOL presents a new approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is tied to its potential impact on molecular design and optimization. By demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on the PMO-1K benchmark, the authors show that MT-MOL offers a practical improvement over existing methods. Furthermore, the emphasis on interpretability and tool-grounded reasoning can lead to more transparent and reliable molecular design processes. The framework's ability to incorporate domain-specific tools can encourage the development of specialized molecular design systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined multi-agent architecture with clear role specifications.
    *   Comprehensive integration of RDKit tools, enabling domain-specific reasoning.
    *   Demonstrated state-of-the-art performance on a standard benchmark (PMO-1K).
    *   Emphasis on interpretability and tool-grounded feedback.
    *   Clear experimental setup and ablation studies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on RDKit, while beneficial, could limit generalization to chemical spaces beyond its coverage.
    *   The computational cost of the multi-agent architecture is mentioned as a potential limitation, but it's not quantified in the experiments. Deeper scalability experiments are required
    *   The prompts provided for the models are not especially advanced and are easily replicable

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM-based molecular design by showcasing the benefits of structured reasoning, multi-agent collaboration, and tool integration. The MT-MOL framework provides a valuable template for building more sophisticated and reliable molecular optimization systems.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper presents a well-engineered system with strong experimental validation.  The results are compelling, showing a clear improvement over existing baselines. The key strength is the systematic approach to incorporating chemical knowledge and ensuring consistency through specialized agents.

### MedSentry: Understanding and Mitigating Safety Risks in Medical LLM Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20824v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Chen, Taihang Zhen, Hewei Wang, Kailai Liu, Xinfeng Li, Jing Huo, Tianpei Yang, Jinfeng Xu, Wei Dong, Yang Gao
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in healthcare, ensuring their safety, particularly within collaborative multi-agent configurations, is paramount. In this paper we introduce MedSentry, a benchmark comprising 5 000 adversarial medical prompts spanning 25 threat categories with 100 subthemes. Coupled with this dataset, we develop an end-to-end attack-defense evaluation pipeline to systematically analyze how four representative multi-agent topologies (Layers, SharedPool, Centralized, and Decentralized) withstand attacks from 'dark-personality' agents. Our findings reveal critical differences in how these architectures handle information contamination and maintain robust decision-making, exposing their underlying vulnerability mechanisms. For instance, SharedPool's open information sharing makes it highly susceptible, whereas Decentralized architectures exhibit greater resilience thanks to inherent redundancy and isolation. To mitigate these risks, we propose a personality-scale detection and correction mechanism that identifies and rehabilitates malicious agents, restoring system safety to near-baseline levels. MedSentry thus furnishes both a rigorous evaluation framework and practical defense strategies that guide the design of safer LLM-based multi-agent systems in medical domains.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the MedSentry paper:

**Summary:**

The MedSentry paper introduces a new benchmark and evaluation framework for assessing and mitigating safety risks in medical Large Language Model (LLM) multi-agent systems (MAS).  The authors create MedSentry, a dataset of 5,000 adversarial medical prompts spanning 25 threat categories.  They then use this dataset to systematically analyze the vulnerabilities of four representative multi-agent topologies (Layers, SharedPool, Centralized, and Decentralized) when exposed to "dark-personality" agents.  The study reveals differences in how these architectures handle information contamination and decision-making, highlighting the vulnerabilities of SharedPool and the relative resilience of Decentralized architectures.  Finally, the paper proposes a lightweight, adaptive defense mechanism based on personality-scale detection and correction (PCDC) to identify and rehabilitate malicious agents, restoring system safety.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper demonstrates good novelty in several aspects:

*   **Benchmark Creation:**  The MedSentry benchmark is a significant contribution. Creating a dedicated, fine-grained dataset focused on insider threats within medical LLM MAS fills a gap in existing benchmarks, which primarily focus on single-agent performance or external adversarial attacks. The detailed topic/subtopic breakdown and efforts to create subtle yet dangerous prompts show careful design.
*   **Architectural Comparison:**  The systematic comparison of different multi-agent topologies concerning their inherent safety properties is novel and provides valuable insights.  The results are not entirely predictable (e.g., the significant vulnerability of the SharedPool architecture), suggesting the analysis uncovers previously unknown risks.
*   **Lightweight Defense Mechanism:** The PCDC approach is relatively novel in the context of medical LLM MAS. The personality-based detection component offers a potentially efficient way to address compromised agents without requiring extensive retraining.

**Significance:**

*   **Practical Relevance:**  The findings have significant practical implications for designing safer medical LLM systems.  The identified vulnerabilities of specific architectures can directly inform design choices.
*   **Direction for Future Research:**  The MedSentry framework provides a solid foundation for future research on adversarial robustness in collaborative medical AI. It offers a standardized platform for evaluating new defense mechanisms and architectural designs.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** By directly addressing the safety risks associated with LLMs in healthcare, the paper contributes to the responsible development and deployment of these technologies.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental setup is thorough, covering multiple architectures, attack strategies, and evaluation metrics. The exploration of the impact of debate rounds, agent numbers, and token ranges adds depth to the analysis.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the research questions, methodology, results, and implications.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors emphasize open access to the code and data, promoting reproducibility and collaboration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Model Reliance:** The primary experiments rely on GPT-4o. While the authors demonstrate some generality by testing other models in a limited context, more extensive evaluation across a wider range of LLMs would strengthen the findings.
*   **Simulated Environment:** The study is conducted in a simulated environment, which might not fully capture the complexities of real-world medical settings. The assumptions made during case-study generation and ethical evaluations could lead to overly optimistic assessments.
*   **PCDC Limitations:** The PCDC defense is a good first step, but it has limitations. It is unlikely to be foolproof against highly sophisticated adversaries who can mimic safe behavior until a critical moment. The reliance on fixed thresholds might require careful tuning for specific applications. The experiments on the effect of the adversarial agent gaining knowledge of the Enforcer Agents inner workings are of interest, but could be expanded to consider agents that adapt their malicious behavior to overcome it, to improve assessment of how easily this defense mechanism can be overcome.
*   **Generalizability from simulated discussions:** Multi agent interactions and dynamics are complex. There is a risk that certain multi-agent collaboration behaviors or specific edge cases are not adequately captured in the simulations provided, which could lead to overconfidence on defense mechanism effectiveness.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the MedSentry paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of medical AI safety. The benchmark dataset, architectural comparison, and proposed defense mechanism are all novel and have practical significance. While the study has some limitations related to the scope of model evaluation and simulation fidelity, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The paper opens avenues for future research and provides practical guidance for designing safer medical LLM systems. Considering the good novelty and potentially high future impact, I would award it a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reinforced Informativeness Optimization for Long-Form Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20825v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Wang, Ruiyang Ren, Yucheng Wang, Wayne Xin Zhao, Jing Liu, Hua Wu, Haifeng Wang
- **Abstract**: Long-form question answering (LFQA) presents unique challenges for large language models, requiring the synthesis of coherent, paragraph-length answers. While retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems have emerged as a promising solution, existing research struggles with key limitations: the scarcity of high-quality training data for long-form generation, the compounding risk of hallucination in extended outputs, and the absence of reliable evaluation metrics for factual completeness. In this paper, we propose RioRAG, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework that advances long-form RAG through reinforced informativeness optimization. Our approach introduces two fundamental innovations to address the core challenges. First, we develop an RL training paradigm of reinforced informativeness optimization that directly optimizes informativeness and effectively addresses the slow-thinking deficit in conventional RAG systems, bypassing the need for expensive supervised data. Second, we propose a nugget-centric hierarchical reward modeling approach that enables precise assessment of long-form answers through a three-stage process: extracting the nugget from every source webpage, constructing a nugget claim checklist, and computing rewards based on factual alignment. Extensive experiments on two LFQA benchmarks LongFact and RAGChecker demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. Our codes are available at https://github.com/RUCAIBox/RioRAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Reinforced Informativeness Optimization for Long-Form Retrieval-Augmented Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RioRAG, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework for long-form question answering (LFQA) using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). It addresses challenges like the scarcity of high-quality training data, the risk of hallucination in long outputs, and the difficulty in evaluating factual completeness. The two main contributions are: (1) a reinforced informativeness optimization RL training paradigm that directly optimizes for information coverage, and (2) a nugget-centric hierarchical reward modeling approach for precise assessment of long-form answers. The method extracts nuggets from web pages, constructs a nugget claim checklist, and computes rewards based on factual alignment. Experiments on LongFact and RAGChecker benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of RioRAG compared to other methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Real Problem:** The paper tackles significant challenges in LFQA-RAG, specifically regarding training data scarcity, hallucination, and evaluation complexity.
*   **Novel Approach:** The reinforced informativeness optimization is a well-motivated and clever solution to the data scarcity problem, allowing the model to learn without relying on expensive human-annotated long-form answers. This contrasts with methods reliant on supervised learning or human feedback.
*   **Nugget-Centric Reward Modeling:** The hierarchical reward modeling provides a structured and interpretable way to evaluate long-form generation quality. This approach is more granular than standard metrics and helps in pinpointing areas for improvement.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments on two recognized benchmarks provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of RioRAG. The ablation studies convincingly demonstrate the importance of each component of the framework.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-organized and easy to follow, making the contributions readily understandable.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Existing LLMs:** The approach builds on top of existing LLMs and the retriever. The performance is thus highly dependent on the base LLM and the quality of web search. While the paper addresses *generation*, the retriever side of the equation isn't optimized.
*   **Generality of Nuggets:** The definition and automatic extraction of "nuggets" are critical to the reward function, yet the paper provides limited details on this process. The robustness and transferability of this component to different datasets or domains may be questionable. How robust are the nugget extraction prompts? Does it extract multiple nuggets from one document.
*   **Limited Novelty:** While the combination of RL and nugget-based evaluation is innovative, the individual components (RL for sequence generation, nugget extraction for QA) are not entirely novel. The novelty lies in the integrated framework and its application to LFQA-RAG.
*   **Evaluation Limitations:** While the paper uses robust benchmarks, LLM-as-a-judge can be unstable.
*   **Practical Implementation Concerns:** The hierarchical reward modelling might involve a higher computational complexity, and the choice of models or training resources could make it less attainable for wider users.

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of LFQA-RAG. By addressing the limitations of existing approaches, RioRAG offers a promising direction for developing more reliable and informative long-form generation systems. The nugget-centric reward modeling may also influence future evaluation methodologies in this area.

**Justification for the Score:**

Despite the limitations, the paper presents a well-designed and empirically validated framework that addresses a significant problem in LFQA-RAG. The reinforced informativeness optimization and the nugget-centric reward modeling are novel and contribute to the field. The strong experimental results support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The weaknesses, like reliance on external LLMs and the potential for bias in nugget extraction, are acknowledged and can be addressed in future work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AdParaphrase v2.0: Generating Attractive Ad Texts Using a Preference-Annotated Paraphrase Dataset
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20826v1)
- **Authors**: Soichiro Murakami, Peinan Zhang, Hidetaka Kamigaito, Hiroya Takamura, Manabu Okumura
- **Abstract**: Identifying factors that make ad text attractive is essential for advertising success. This study proposes AdParaphrase v2.0, a dataset for ad text paraphrasing, containing human preference data, to enable the analysis of the linguistic factors and to support the development of methods for generating attractive ad texts. Compared with v1.0, this dataset is 20 times larger, comprising 16,460 ad text paraphrase pairs, each annotated with preference data from ten evaluators, thereby enabling a more comprehensive and reliable analysis. Through the experiments, we identified multiple linguistic features of engaging ad texts that were not observed in v1.0 and explored various methods for generating attractive ad texts. Furthermore, our analysis demonstrated the relationships between human preference and ad performance, and highlighted the potential of reference-free metrics based on large language models for evaluating ad text attractiveness. The dataset is publicly available at: https://github.com/CyberAgentAILab/AdParaphrase-v2.0.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ADPARAPHRASE V2.0, an enhanced dataset for generating attractive ad texts. This dataset contains a significantly larger set of ad text paraphrase pairs annotated with human preference data compared to its predecessor (v1.0). This expansion enables a more comprehensive analysis of linguistic factors influencing ad text attractiveness and supports the development of better ad text generation (ATG) methods. The authors detail the data collection process, which involves using large language models (LLMs), crowdsourcing, and manual annotation. They also present experimental results identifying linguistic features of engaging ad texts and evaluate various methods for generating such texts, including in-context learning (ICL), instruction tuning, and preference tuning. Furthermore, they analyze the relationship between human preference and ad performance, and explore reference-free metrics for evaluating ad text attractiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the significantly expanded dataset, ADPARAPHRASE V2.0. While the original dataset existed, the 20-fold increase in size is substantial.  The use of LLMs and crowdsourcing to create the dataset isn't entirely novel, but the scale and careful annotation with human preferences are noteworthy.  The analysis of a broader range of linguistic features compared to v1.0, including previously unobserved ones, adds incremental novelty.  The exploration of preference tuning and instruction tuning techniques for ATG, beyond ICL, provides some additional value. However, the techniques themselves are not entirely new.

**Significance:** The expanded dataset and analysis of linguistic features has potential significance for:

*   **ATG research:** The dataset provides a valuable resource for training and evaluating ATG models, especially those aimed at generating persuasive and engaging ad copy.
*   **Linguistic understanding:** The identified linguistic features offer insights into the factors that make ad texts attractive to humans.
*   **Ad optimization:** The analysis of the relationship between human preference and ad performance could inform strategies for optimizing real-world ad campaigns.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** The assessment of reference-free metrics opens the door to more efficient automatic evaluation of generated ad texts.

**Strengths:**

*   **Dataset scale:** The increased size of ADPARAPHRASE v2.0 addresses a key limitation of the original dataset.
*   **Detailed data collection process:** The paper clearly outlines the methodology for data collection, annotation, and quality control.
*   **Comprehensive analysis:** The experiments cover a range of linguistic features and ATG methods.
*   **Practical implications:** The findings have potential applications in real-world advertising.
*   **Publicly available dataset:** The open availability of the dataset encourages further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited novelty of methods:**  While the application of ICL, instruction tuning, and preference tuning to the specific problem of ad text generation has value, the methods themselves are well-established in NLP.
*   **Potential biases in data:** The data is based on Japanese ad texts and Japanese evaluators, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other languages and cultures.  The study acknowledges this limitation.
*   **Subjectivity of preference judgments:** Human preference is inherently subjective, and while the authors took steps to mitigate bias, this remains a factor that could influence the results.
*   **Over-reliance on LLMs:** Much of the dataset generation relies on LLMs. While these models are powerful, they also tend to encode certain biases and stylistic tendencies, potentially influencing the nature of the dataset.
*   **Limited evaluation of reference-free metrics:** While explored, the analysis demonstrating that existing methods can fill this role might benefit from more examples and thorough discussion.
*   **Modest online evaluation:** The A/B testing experiments, although providing real-world validation, are relatively limited. It may be necessary to increase the amount of data available for future work.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign this paper a **Score: 6**.

**Rationale:**

The paper's strength lies in the creation and release of a substantially improved dataset that directly addresses limitations of its predecessor. This provides a valuable resource for the community. The analysis of linguistic features is thorough and insightful. However, the application of ATG methods is more incremental than revolutionary. While the findings have practical implications, the potential for widespread impact is tempered by the language-specific nature of the dataset and the reliance on LLMs, as the impact would need to be replicated in more languages and settings for a truly valuable contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Frame-Level Captions for Long Video Generation with Complex Multi Scenes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20827v1)
- **Authors**: Guangcong Zheng, Jianlong Yuan, Bo Wang, Haoyang Huang, Guoqing Ma, Nan Duan
- **Abstract**: Generating long videos that can show complex stories, like movie scenes from scripts, has great promise and offers much more than short clips. However, current methods that use autoregression with diffusion models often struggle because their step-by-step process naturally leads to a serious error accumulation (drift). Also, many existing ways to make long videos focus on single, continuous scenes, making them less useful for stories with many events and changes. This paper introduces a new approach to solve these problems. First, we propose a novel way to annotate datasets at the frame-level, providing detailed text guidance needed for making complex, multi-scene long videos. This detailed guidance works with a Frame-Level Attention Mechanism to make sure text and video match precisely. A key feature is that each part (frame) within these windows can be guided by its own distinct text prompt. Our training uses Diffusion Forcing to provide the model with the ability to handle time flexibly. We tested our approach on difficult VBench 2.0 benchmarks ("Complex Plots" and "Complex Landscapes") based on the WanX2.1-T2V-1.3B model. The results show our method is better at following instructions in complex, changing scenes and creates high-quality long videos. We plan to share our dataset annotation methods and trained models with the research community. Project page: https://zgctroy.github.io/frame-level-captions .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of generating long, complex videos from text descriptions, focusing on semantic consistency and mitigating error accumulation.  The core contributions include:

1.  **Frame-Level Dataset Methodology:**  A method for creating datasets with dense, frame-by-frame textual annotations.
2.  **Frame-Level Attention:** An attention mechanism that links each video segment's visual features to its specific frame-level text prompt.
3.  **Parallel Multi-Window Denoising (PMWD):** An inference strategy that processes long videos as multiple overlapping windows denoised simultaneously in parallel, leveraging Diffusion Forcing and frame-level prompts to reduce error accumulation.
4.  Evaluations on VBench 2.0 ("Complex Plots" and "Complex Landscapes") using the WanX2.1-T2V-1.3B model.

The authors argue that their frame-level approach is superior to global or shot-level captioning and that PMWD addresses the error accumulation issues inherent in autoregressive video generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of using frame-level annotations for video generation isn't entirely new. While the specific implementation of frame-level attention might have some novelty, it builds upon existing attention mechanisms.  PMWD appears to be a more significant contribution, representing a departure from purely sequential video generation. The combination of the three components, however, represents a genuinely novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a critical problem in video generation: generating long, coherent narratives. Error accumulation and semantic drift are major obstacles to achieving this goal. If PMWD is effective, it could have a substantial impact on the field. The benchmark results presented offer preliminary support, however they are still insufficient.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition.
    *   Well-structured approach with distinct components.
    *   Addressing a significant limitation of existing autoregressive methods.
    *   Empirical evaluation on challenging benchmarks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited quantitative evaluation.  VBench 2.0 is a valuable benchmark, but more comprehensive metrics and ablations would strengthen the claims. The "Confusion Degree" metric is interesting but needs more justification and perhaps comparison to other metrics. Furthermore, comparing against other leading methods (other than just video-level prompting baselines) is required to demonstrate the superior performance of the approach.
    *   Lack of detailed ablation studies. It's unclear how much each component (frame-level attention, Diffusion Forcing, and PMWD) contributes to the overall performance. This makes it difficult to assess the importance of each part and could potentially hide redundant elements.
    * The manuscript contains numerous grammatical errors and the writing style is not professional.
    * The visualization of the key frames in relation to the story being generated is inadequate. A video showing the results would allow a better evaluation of the method.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the method proves to be robust and generalizable, it could influence future research in long video generation and potentially be adopted by other researchers. The PMWD technique, in particular, may be a useful alternative to sequential generation. The frame-level annotation approach could also inspire the creation of new datasets.

* The qualitative results are lackluster, with considerable room for improvement.

* The paper includes several novel contributions, but due to the limitations listed above, the significance is reduced.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents an interesting approach to a challenging problem. The PMWD technique and the frame-level attention mechanism are potentially valuable contributions. However, the limited scope of the experiments, the lack of detailed ablations, comparison against SOTA methods, and a few shortcomings in writing limit the overall significance of the work. It is unlikely to have a substantial impact to the field.

- **Score**: 5/10

### GET: Goal-directed Exploration and Targeting for Large-Scale Unknown Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20828v1)
- **Authors**: Lanxiang Zheng, Ruidong Mei, Mingxin Wei, Hao Ren, Hui Cheng
- **Abstract**: Object search in large-scale, unstructured environments remains a fundamental challenge in robotics, particularly in dynamic or expansive settings such as outdoor autonomous exploration. This task requires robust spatial reasoning and the ability to leverage prior experiences. While Large Language Models (LLMs) offer strong semantic capabilities, their application in embodied contexts is limited by a grounding gap in spatial reasoning and insufficient mechanisms for memory integration and decision consistency.To address these challenges, we propose GET (Goal-directed Exploration and Targeting), a framework that enhances object search by combining LLM-based reasoning with experience-guided exploration. At its core is DoUT (Diagram of Unified Thought), a reasoning module that facilitates real-time decision-making through a role-based feedback loop, integrating task-specific criteria and external memory. For repeated tasks, GET maintains a probabilistic task map based on a Gaussian Mixture Model, allowing for continual updates to object-location priors as environments evolve.Experiments conducted in real-world, large-scale environments demonstrate that GET improves search efficiency and robustness across multiple LLMs and task settings, significantly outperforming heuristic and LLM-only baselines. These results suggest that structured LLM integration provides a scalable and generalizable approach to embodied decision-making in complex environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GET (Goal-directed Exploration and Targeting), a novel framework for object search in large-scale, unknown environments. GET integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) for semantic reasoning with experience-guided exploration. Its core component, DoUT (Diagram of Unified Thought), is a reasoning module that uses a role-based feedback loop to integrate task-specific criteria and external memory for real-time decision-making. GET also incorporates a Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM)-based probabilistic task map to continually update object location priors as the environment evolves. The paper presents real-world experiments demonstrating improved search efficiency and robustness compared to heuristic and LLM-only baselines, suggesting that structured LLM integration is a scalable and generalizable approach to embodied decision-making in complex environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the structured integration of LLMs, a novel reasoning module (DoUT), and a probabilistic experience model (GMM) for object search. While LLMs have been used in robotics before, the specific architecture and the way GET combines these components seem new. DoUT, in particular, is presented as a contribution to improve LLM-based reasoning. The GMM-based task map provides a memory mechanism not always present in LLM-based robotic systems. The authors propose a novel end-to-end framwork with real-time updates, which is highly effective in real-world scenarios.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in robotics: efficient and robust object search in large, unstructured environments. The results indicate GET outperforms existing approaches, suggesting potential for real-world applications such as search and rescue, autonomous exploration, and service robotics. A main issue with many LLM applications is their "black box" nature and lack of robustness. By structuring the LLM's decision process with DoUT and introducing a memory component, GET tackles this issue and makes the system more reliable and interpretable.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Integration:** The combination of LLM reasoning, real-time feedback, and experience modeling is a strong point.
    * **Real-World Validation:** Experiments in large-scale, real-world environments provide convincing evidence of GET's effectiveness.
    * **Detailed Architecture:** The paper provides a thorough description of the GET framework, including the DoUT and GMM components, facilitating reproducibility.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The results demonstrate a significant improvement compared to baselines, suggesting a real advancement in the field.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **LLM Reliance:** The performance depends on the LLM’s capabilities. It is not clear from the paper how the authors dealt with hallucinations or incorrect information provided by LLM.
    * **Computational Cost:** A more thorough discussion of the computational requirements, including the impact of different LLMs and the overhead of DoUT and the GMM, would be beneficial. While the paper claims real-time performance, more details on the hardware and processing times would be helpful.

* **Potential Influence:** If the code and models are released, this paper could have significant influence. The modular nature of the GET framework would allow other researchers to replace and improve individual components. The DoUT and GMM components could find use in other areas of robotic and AI decision-making. The real-world results showcase the potential for applications outside the laboratory.

* **Rigorous Evaluation:** The performance metrics of the proposed method are well-defined, and the ablation studies indicate the performance benefits of each component of GET, compared to the baseline methods Sem, FAEL, EFP and TARE.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a well-designed and validated system that improves upon previous techniques for object search in complex environments. The integration of LLMs with a memory module is well motivated. The real-world experiments strengthen the claim. The architecture is reasonable and clearly explained.

### FireQ: Fast INT4-FP8 Kernel and RoPE-aware Quantization for LLM Inference Acceleration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20839v1)
- **Authors**: Daehyeon Baek, Jieun Choi, Jimyoung Son, Kyungmin Bin, Seungbeom Choi, Kihyo Moon, Minsung Jang, Hyojung Lee
- **Abstract**: As large language models become increasingly prevalent, memory bandwidth constraints significantly limit inference throughput, motivating post-training quantization (PTQ). In this paper, we propose FireQ, a co-designed PTQ framework and an INT4-FP8 matrix multiplication kernel that accelerates LLM inference across all linear layers. Specifically, FireQ quantizes linear layer weights and key-values to INT4, and activations and queries to FP8, significantly enhancing throughput. Additionally, we introduce a three-stage pipelining for the prefill phase, which modifies the FlashAttention-3 kernel, effectively reducing time-to-first-token in the prefill phase. To minimize accuracy loss from quantization, we develop novel outlier smoothing techniques tailored separately for linear and attention layers. In linear layers, we explicitly use per-tensor scaling to prevent underflow caused by the FP8 quantization scaling factor of INT4 quantization, and channel-wise scaling to compensate for coarse granularity of INT4. In attention layers, we address quantization challenges posed by rotary positional embeddings (RoPE) by combining pre-RoPE and post-RoPE scaling strategies. FireQ significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving 1.68x faster inference in feed-forward network layers on Llama2-7B and 1.26x faster prefill phase performance on Llama3-8B compared to QServe, with negligible accuracy loss.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FireQ paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FireQ, a framework for accelerating Large Language Model (LLM) inference through a co-designed post-training quantization (PTQ) strategy and an optimized INT4-FP8 matrix multiplication kernel. FireQ quantizes linear layer weights and key-values to INT4, and activations and queries to FP8. It includes novel outlier smoothing techniques tailored for linear and attention layers, addressing underflow issues caused by FP8 scaling factors and challenges posed by rotary positional embeddings (RoPE). The prefill phase is accelerated using a three-stage pipelining approach modifying the FlashAttention-3 kernel. The authors claim FireQ outperforms existing methods like QServe and TensorRT-LLM in terms of inference speed with minimal accuracy loss.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Kernel Optimization:** The INT4-FP8 kernel is a solid engineering contribution. Leveraging CUDA cores for in-register dequantization is a promising approach to improving throughput. The adoption of FP8 tensor cores is well aligned with hardware trends and enables better utilization of Hopper GPUs.
    *   **RoPE-aware Quantization:** Addressing the challenges RoPE introduces to quantization is a valuable contribution. The two-stage outlier smoothing (RPN and CRS) demonstrates a nuanced approach.
    *   **Prefill Acceleration:** Modifying FlashAttention-3 with the three-stage pipeline is a reasonable optimization for the prefill phase, reducing time-to-first-token. The producer-consumer warpgroup structure and use of TMA are likely to improve hardware utilization.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes results on multiple LLMs (Llama2, Llama3), compares against multiple baselines, and uses various metrics (perplexity, zero-shot performance, throughput).  The ablation study on quantization strategies gives more insight.
    *   **Good writing style with reasonable illustrations:** Figures provide a reasonable degree of illustration for the main concepts proposed in this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is effective, individual components appear to be incremental improvements over existing methods.  The core ideas (INT4/FP8 mixed precision, RoPE handling, FlashAttention modifications) are not entirely new. It appears they adopt the best of the past few years.
    *   **Limited Justification for FP8 Choice:** While FP8 aligns with hardware, the paper could provide a more in-depth justification for choosing FP8 over other low-precision formats for activations, showing where it has better properties.
    *   **Lack of Error Bounds:** The paper reports negligible accuracy loss, but it would be more compelling if it could offer some kind of theoretical or empirical bounds on the accuracy degradation due to quantization.
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:** Without released code and model weights, it is difficult to fully verify the results. This is a common issue, but it does impact the assessment.
    *   **Hardware Specific:** The optimizations are heavily tied to NVIDIA Hopper GPUs and may not generalize well to other hardware platforms. This limits the broad applicability of the work.

*   **Significance:**
    *   FireQ contributes to the ongoing effort to make LLM inference more efficient. The combination of kernel optimization and quantization strategies is relevant and timely.
    *   The RoPE-aware quantization techniques could be useful to the community for working with RoPE-based models.
    *   The paper's approach demonstrates the potential of mixed-precision quantization and hardware-aware optimization.

*   **Justification of Score:**

FireQ represents a well-engineered system that leverages existing ideas and hardware features to achieve significant performance improvements in LLM inference. The paper's strength lies in its careful integration of multiple techniques and its thorough evaluation. However, its reliance on specific hardware and the incremental nature of its core ideas limit its overall novelty and impact. Considering these factors, a score of **7** is appropriate. It is a solid engineering contribution that achieves practical improvements but does not offer breakthrough innovations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Concealment of Intent: A Game-Theoretic Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20841v1)
- **Authors**: Xinbo Wu, Abhishek Umrawal, Lav R. Varshney
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) grow more capable, concerns about their safe deployment have also grown. Although alignment mechanisms have been introduced to deter misuse, they remain vulnerable to carefully designed adversarial prompts. In this work, we present a scalable attack strategy: intent-hiding adversarial prompting, which conceals malicious intent through the composition of skills. We develop a game-theoretic framework to model the interaction between such attacks and defense systems that apply both prompt and response filtering. Our analysis identifies equilibrium points and reveals structural advantages for the attacker. To counter these threats, we propose and analyze a defense mechanism tailored to intent-hiding attacks. Empirically, we validate the attack's effectiveness on multiple real-world LLMs across a range of malicious behaviors, demonstrating clear advantages over existing adversarial prompting techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack strategy called "intent-hiding adversarial prompting" against large language models (LLMs). This attack aims to bypass prompt and response filtering by concealing malicious intent through the strategic combination of skills. The authors model the interaction between the attacker and the defender (with prompt and response filtering) using a game-theoretic framework. This framework allows them to identify equilibrium points and show structural advantages for the attacker.  They also propose and analyze a defense mechanism tailored to counter intent-hiding attacks.  Empirical results on real-world LLMs demonstrate the effectiveness of the attack and validate the theoretical findings, showing superior performance compared to existing adversarial prompting techniques. The authors further validate the defense mechanism's capability in mitigating the proposed attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of hiding intent through skill composition is a valuable and novel contribution to the field of LLM security. It moves beyond simple prompt manipulation and considers a more nuanced approach to circumventing safety mechanisms. The game-theoretic analysis provides a formal framework for understanding these attacks, which is less common in the literature.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical concern: the vulnerability of LLMs to sophisticated adversarial attacks.  Demonstrating that malicious intent can be concealed effectively, even with prompt and response filtering, highlights a significant gap in current LLM defense strategies.  The practical implications of the game-theoretic analysis, particularly the scalability of the attack with larger skill spaces, are also significant. The work introduces a tailored defense mechanism, providing concrete directions for future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Theoretical Foundation:** The game-theoretic framework provides a rigorous foundation for analyzing the attack and defense dynamics.
    *   **Effective Attack Strategy:** Empirical results demonstrate the attack's effectiveness against multiple LLMs, surpassing existing methods.
    *   **Practical Implications:**  The analysis reveals insights into scaling attack performance and informs the design of effective defenses.
    *   **Tailored defense Mechanism**: The work introduces a defense method tailored to address the proposed attack.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplified Assumptions:** The theoretical model makes simplifying assumptions (e.g., uniform utility, fixed E, M, J functions) that might limit the direct applicability of the results to real-world scenarios. The assumption of a known prior intent distribution may also not hold in reality.
    *   **Limited Skill Set:** The experiments use a relatively small set of predefined skills. Exploring a broader and more dynamic skill space would strengthen the empirical validation.
    *   **Defense Mechanism Implementation Details**: The work could benefit from a more detailed explanation and implementation specifics of the developed defense mechanism.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could significantly impact the research community by:

    *   Motivating further investigation into intent-hiding attacks and the development of more robust defenses.
    *   Encouraging the use of game-theoretic frameworks for analyzing LLM security.
    *   Informing the design of LLM safety mechanisms that are more resilient to complex adversarial prompts.
    *   Offering a clear roadmap on how to improve the robustness of prompt and response filtering systems.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a valuable and timely contribution to the field of LLM security. While the theoretical framework and empirical evaluation have limitations, the core idea of intent-hiding attacks and the resulting insights are significant. The identified defense strategies is also a significant direction of study.

### An LLM-as-Judge Metric for Bridging the Gap with Human Evaluation in SE Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20854v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Zhou, Kisub Kim, Ting Zhang, Martin Weyssow, Luis F. Gomes, Guang Yang, David Lo
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) and other automated techniques have been increasingly used to support software developers by generating software artifacts such as code snippets, patches, and comments. However, accurately assessing the correctness of these generated artifacts remains a significant challenge. On one hand, human evaluation provides high accuracy but is labor-intensive and lacks scalability. On the other hand, other existing automatic evaluation metrics are scalable and require minimal human effort, but they often fail to accurately reflect the actual correctness of generated software artifacts. In this paper, we present SWE-Judge, the first evaluation metric for LLM-as-Ensemble-Judge specifically designed to accurately assess the correctness of generated software artifacts. SWE-Judge first defines five distinct evaluation strategies, each implemented as an independent judge. A dynamic team selection mechanism then identifies the most appropriate subset of judges to produce a final correctness score through ensembling. We evaluate SWE-Judge across a diverse set of software engineering (SE) benchmarks, including CoNaLa, Card2Code, HumanEval-X, APPS, APR-Assess, and Summary-Assess. These benchmarks span three SE tasks: code generation, automated program repair, and code summarization. Experimental results demonstrate that SWE-Judge consistently achieves a higher correlation with human judgments, with improvements ranging from 5.9% to 183.8% over existing automatic metrics. Furthermore, SWE-Judge reaches agreement levels with human annotators that are comparable to inter-annotator agreement in code generation and program repair tasks. These findings underscore SWE-Judge's potential as a scalable and reliable alternative to human evaluation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SWE-Judge, a novel evaluation metric that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) as ensemble judges to assess the correctness of software artifacts. It addresses the limitations of existing evaluation methods, such as the high cost and limited scalability of human evaluation, and the inaccuracies of automated metrics that rely on simple similarity or test case based analysis. SWE-Judge uses a multi-evaluator approach with five distinct evaluation strategies, and a dynamic team selection mechanism to choose the best combination of strategies for a given dataset. The individual assessments are then combined through ensembling to produce a final correctness score.  The authors evaluate SWE-Judge on several software engineering benchmarks spanning code generation, automated program repair, and code summarization tasks, across multiple programming languages.  The results show that SWE-Judge achieves higher correlations with human judgments compared to existing automatic evaluation metrics, and in some cases reaches agreement levels comparable to inter-annotator agreement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a real and significant problem:**  Evaluating the correctness of generated software is a crucial and challenging task. The paper clearly articulates the shortcomings of current methods.
    *   **Novelty:** The LLM-as-ensemble-judge approach is a genuinely innovative way to leverage the power of LLMs for software engineering tasks. The dynamic team selection mechanism adds further value by adapting to the specific characteristics of different datasets. The strategies are novel and well considered.
    *   **Strong empirical validation:**  The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation across a diverse set of benchmarks and programming languages. The results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of SWE-Judge over existing metrics. The ablation study provides insights into the importance of the different components. It would have been interesting to understand what teams were chosen for each dataset, but this does not detract from the core argument.
    *   **Detailed explanation and organization:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology and experimental setup. The inclusion of example prompts is valuable for reproducibility.
    *   **Code is available.** This is a huge bonus to reproduce and extend this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The approach inherently depends on the capabilities and biases of the underlying LLM. There could be scenarios where the LLM fails to adequately assess the correctness of certain types of software artifacts. If the LLM is poor, then the results will be poor. However, they used a fairly powerful LLM to try and mitigate this.
    *   **Computational cost:** While the authors selected a lightweight LLM, the computational cost of running multiple LLM evaluations (even with a more cost-effective model) can still be significant, particularly for large datasets. No concrete cost numbers are presented in this paper.
    *   **Limited investigation of individual strategy performance:** The paper focuses on the overall performance of SWE-Judge. It could have been strengthened by a more in-depth analysis of the individual evaluation strategies. What were the strengths and limitations of each strategy, and why did certain combinations work better than others? Also, an analysis of what teams were chosen, would have helped understand the method.
    *   **Relatively simple ensembling:** The averaging strategy for combining the individual scores is relatively simple. It could potentially be improved by using more sophisticated ensembling techniques or by weighting the different strategies based on their individual performance. This simplicity may mean it generalises better though.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of software engineering by providing a more scalable and accurate way to evaluate the correctness of generated software. SWE-Judge could be used to improve the development of automated software engineering tools, and to help developers assess the quality of code generated by LLMs. It has the potential to lead to more efficient development workflows by removing the need for manual checking of code.

*   **Justification:** The paper presents a sound evaluation of an important idea, with strong performance across a range of tasks. The use of an ensemble and team selection mechanism gives it a clear advantage over previous approaches. However, the reliance on LLMs and limited costings mean it is not perfect.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a novel and well-validated method for evaluating software artifacts that bridges the gap between human evaluations and existing automated methods. SWE-Judge demonstrates significant improvements over baselines across a range of tasks. Although it relies on LLMs which are inherently expensive, and could be improved through the costing of the whole process, the performance gains warrant a high rating.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### G-DReaM: Graph-conditioned Diffusion Retargeting across Multiple Embodiments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20857v1)
- **Authors**: Zhefeng Cao, Ben Liu, Sen Li, Wei Zhang, Hua Chen
- **Abstract**: Motion retargeting for specific robot from existing motion datasets is one critical step in transferring motion patterns from human behaviors to and across various robots. However, inconsistencies in topological structure, geometrical parameters as well as joint correspondence make it difficult to handle diverse embodiments with a unified retargeting architecture. In this work, we propose a novel unified graph-conditioned diffusion-based motion generation framework for retargeting reference motions across diverse embodiments. The intrinsic characteristics of heterogeneous embodiments are represented with graph structure that effectively captures topological and geometrical features of different robots. Such a graph-based encoding further allows for knowledge exploitation at the joint level with a customized attention mechanisms developed in this work. For lacking ground truth motions of the desired embodiment, we utilize an energy-based guidance formulated as retargeting losses to train the diffusion model. As one of the first cross-embodiment motion retargeting methods in robotics, our experiments validate that the proposed model can retarget motions across heterogeneous embodiments in a unified manner. Moreover, it demonstrates a certain degree of generalization to both diverse skeletal structures and similar motion patterns.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces G-DReaM, a novel graph-conditioned diffusion-based framework for motion retargeting across diverse robot embodiments. The method addresses the challenge of transferring motion patterns between robots with different skeletal structures and kinematic properties. G-DReaM uses a graph structure to represent the intrinsic characteristics of each robot, capturing topological and geometrical features.  It leverages a transformer-based diffusion model, conditioned on the graph representation and a reference motion, to generate retargeted motions.  An energy-based guidance, formulated as retargeting losses, is used to train the diffusion model in the absence of paired motion data for the target embodiment.  Experiments demonstrate the model's ability to retarget motions across heterogeneous robots and generalize to new skeletal structures and similar motion patterns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its unified approach to cross-embodiment motion retargeting using a graph-conditioned diffusion model. Existing methods are often tailored to specific robot pairs or require paired motion data. G-DReaM is among the first to leverage diffusion models for this task and to explicitly encode robot morphology as a graph structure within this framework.  The attention mechanism modifications and the use of an energy-based loss function to guide the diffusion process in the absence of paired data also contribute to its novelty.
*   **Significance:** Motion retargeting is a critical problem in robotics, enabling the transfer of learned behaviors across different robotic platforms.  A unified framework like G-DReaM has the potential to simplify the development of robot control systems and accelerate the deployment of robots in various applications.  The demonstrated ability to generalize to new skeletal structures and motion patterns further enhances the practical value of the method. The paper addresses a key bottleneck in robotics: the robot-specific motion data.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The graph-based representation effectively captures the morphological characteristics of different robots.
    *   The diffusion model provides a flexible and powerful framework for motion generation.
    *   The energy-based guidance allows training without paired motion data.
    *   Experiments demonstrate the model's ability to retarget motions across heterogeneous robots and generalize to new skeletal structures.
    *   The architecture handles variable-length data and incorporates graph structure conditioning through attention mechanisms which is a significant technical achievement.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method relies on manually specified joint correspondences. While the paper mentions future work on learning these correspondences, the current dependency is a limitation.
    *   The paper acknowledges that the generated motions may violate certain kinematic constraints. Addressing these violations is a crucial area for future research.
    *   The experimental evaluation could be more comprehensive. A comparison to other state-of-the-art motion retargeting methods would strengthen the paper's claims. A more rigorous quantitative evaluation of the quality of the retargeted motions (beyond MSE loss) would be valuable, potentially including metrics related to motion smoothness and plausibility.
    *   While the method demonstrates generalization to similar motion patterns, the limitations regarding drastically different motions (e.g., getting up from the ground) suggest the model's reliance on the distribution of training data.
*   **Potential Influence:** G-DReaM has the potential to influence research in motion retargeting, robot control, and imitation learning. It provides a promising framework for transferring motions across diverse robots and could inspire further research in graph-based representations, diffusion models, and energy-based guidance. The insights gained from this work could contribute to the development of more general and adaptable robot control systems.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to a significant problem in robotics. While it has some limitations, its strengths in graph representation, diffusion modeling, and training without paired data, combined with positive experimental results and its potential influence on the field justify a relatively high score.

### AVCD: Mitigating Hallucinations in Audio-Visual Large Language Models through Contrastive Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20862v1)
- **Authors**: Chaeyoung Jung, Youngjoon Jang, Joon Son Chung
- **Abstract**: Hallucination remains a major challenge in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). To address this, various contrastive decoding (CD) methods have been proposed that contrasts original logits with hallucinated logits generated from perturbed inputs. While CD has shown promise in vision-language models (VLMs), it is not well-suited for AV-LLMs, where hallucinations often emerge from both unimodal and cross-modal combinations involving audio, video, and language. These intricate interactions call for a more adaptive and modality-aware decoding strategy. In this paper, we propose Audio-Visual Contrastive Decoding (AVCD)-a novel, training-free decoding framework designed to model trimodal interactions and suppress modality-induced hallucinations in AV-LLMs. Unlike previous CD methods in VLMs that corrupt a fixed modality, AVCD leverages attention distributions to dynamically identify less dominant modalities and applies attentive masking to generate perturbed output logits. To support CD in a trimodal setting, we also reformulate the original CD framework to jointly handle audio, visual, and textual inputs. Finally, to improve efficiency, we introduce entropy-guided adaptive decoding, which selectively skips unnecessary decoding steps based on the model's confidence in its predictions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AVCD consistently outperforms existing decoding methods. Especially, on the AVHBench dataset, it improves accuracy by 6% for VideoLLaMA2 and 11% for video-SALMONN, demonstrating strong robustness and generalizability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Audio-Visual Contrastive Decoding (AVCD), a novel, training-free decoding framework designed to mitigate hallucinations in Audio-Visual Large Language Models (AV-LLMs).  Unlike existing contrastive decoding methods that perturb a fixed modality, AVCD dynamically identifies less dominant modalities by analyzing attention distributions and then applies attentive masking to generate perturbed output logits. This promotes a more balanced reliance on all modalities. The paper also reformulates the original contrastive decoding framework to jointly handle audio, visual, and textual inputs. To improve efficiency, an entropy-guided adaptive decoding mechanism is introduced, skipping decoding steps when the model is confident. Experiments across multiple AV-LLMs and datasets demonstrate that AVCD consistently outperforms existing decoding methods, particularly on the AVHBench dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant improvement over existing contrastive decoding methods, particularly in the context of AV-LLMs. The key innovation lies in the adaptive, modality-aware masking strategy that leverages attention distributions to selectively perturb less dominant modalities. This is a crucial step forward from previous approaches that rely on fixed modality perturbation, which can be sub-optimal for the complex multimodal interactions present in AV-LLMs. The reformulation of the contrastive decoding framework to handle the trimodal setting (audio, visual, text) is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** Hallucination is a major barrier to the reliable deployment of both LLMs and MLLMs.  The paper addresses this problem directly in AV-LLMs, a relatively under-explored but increasingly important area.  The reported improvements in accuracy, especially on the AVHBench dataset (designed specifically to assess audio-visual hallucinations) demonstrates the practical relevance of the method. The fact that AVCD is training-free makes it easily adaptable to existing AV-LLMs without requiring extensive retraining, further enhancing its impact. The entropy guided decoding also addresses efficiency concerns.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Adaptive Modality Masking:** This is the core strength of the paper.  By dynamically identifying and masking less dominant modalities, AVCD avoids the pitfalls of fixed modality perturbation.
    *   **Trimodal Contrastive Formulation:** The adaptation of contrastive decoding to the audio-visual-text setting is well-motivated and effectively addresses the complex interaction of these modalities.
    *   **Training-Free Approach:**  The lack of training requirement makes AVCD practical and easy to integrate with existing AV-LLMs.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results across multiple datasets and models demonstrate the effectiveness and generalizability of AVCD.
    *   **Entropy guided decoding** Addresses efficiency issues of CD.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Attention Distributions:** The method's performance hinges on the accuracy of the attention distributions in the AV-LLM. If these attention distributions are unreliable or biased, AVCD may not perform optimally. While the experiments show effectiveness, a theoretical analysis of how errors in attention distributions affect AVCD's performance would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The choice of entropy threshold, masking ratio, and contrastive weight (α) can potentially influence the performance of AVCD. Although the authors provided some guidelines for parameter selection, a more comprehensive analysis of the hyperparameter space and its impact on performance would be beneficial. Furthermore, the authors mention 70% of the attention is dedicated to the language modality in the paper; this might lead the reader to believe that language is always dominant, which can be a point of confusion.
    *   **Limited Scope of Datasets:** While the paper presents results on several datasets, some specialized audio-visual reasoning or interaction datasets are missing.
    *  The paper does not seem to explore/analyse the causes of hallucinations at low entropies. It would be great to see an analysis of what causes hallucinations even at low entropies, and if AVCD can be adapted to handle those situations.
*   **Potential Influence:** AVCD has the potential to significantly influence the design and application of AV-LLMs.  Its adaptive, training-free nature makes it a valuable tool for mitigating hallucinations and improving the reliability of these models. The paper could spur further research into more sophisticated modality fusion and decoding strategies for multimodal language models.

*   **Justification of Score:** While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution, the limitations mentioned above prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the novelty and empirical results are undeniable, the lack of theoretical analysis concerning attention errors and limited analysis of hyperparameters lowers the overall evaluation.
