### FeatInv: Spatially resolved mapping from feature space to input space using conditional diffusion models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21032v1)
- **Authors**: Nils Neukirch, Johanna Vielhaben, Nils Strodthoff
- **Abstract**: Internal representations are crucial for understanding deep neural networks, such as their properties and reasoning patterns, but remain difficult to interpret. While mapping from feature space to input space aids in interpreting the former, existing approaches often rely on crude approximations. We propose using a conditional diffusion model - a pretrained high-fidelity diffusion model conditioned on spatially resolved feature maps - to learn such a mapping in a probabilistic manner. We demonstrate the feasibility of this approach across various pretrained image classifiers from CNNs to ViTs, showing excellent reconstruction capabilities. Through qualitative comparisons and robustness analysis, we validate our method and showcase possible applications, such as the visualization of concept steering in input space or investigations of the composite nature of the feature space. This approach has broad potential for improving feature space understanding in computer vision models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FeatInv: Spatially resolved mapping from feature space to input space using conditional diffusion models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FeatInv, a method for learning a probabilistic mapping from a deep neural network's feature space back to the input space. It uses a conditional diffusion model, specifically a ControlNet conditioned on spatially resolved feature maps extracted from pretrained CNN/ViT models. The goal is to generate high-fidelity synthetic images whose feature representations closely align with the original image's representation.  The paper demonstrates the feasibility of this approach, showing excellent reconstruction capabilities across different models, and showcases applications like concept steering visualization and investigating the composite nature of feature spaces. The authors highlight the advantage of using spatially resolved feature maps over pooled representations and demonstrate the robustness and quality of the generated samples.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of mapping from feature space to input space is not entirely novel; existing methods use conditional generative models. However, the paper's novelty lies in several aspects:
    *   **Probabilistic Mapping with Spatial Resolution:** Most prior works on feature inversion rely on deterministic mappings or discard the spatial structure of the feature maps through pooling. FeatInv utilizes a state-of-the-art diffusion model *conditioned on spatially resolved* feature maps, which allows it to capture fine-grained details and the uncertainty inherent in this inverse problem. This is a significant step up from previous techniques.
    *   **State-of-the-Art Generative Model:** Using a modern diffusion model (MiniSD) is also a key contribution. It enables much higher fidelity reconstructions than previous attempts.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation with quantitative metrics (cosine similarity, top-k matching, FID score) and qualitative visualizations. The robustness analysis, comparing results across different backbones, also strengthens the paper.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the increased interpretability and potential applications it unlocks:
    *   **Improved Interpretability:** By providing a high-fidelity visualization of how a model perceives input samples, FeatInv can help researchers understand model reasoning patterns and properties.
    *   **Concept Steering:**  The ability to visualize the effects of concept steering in input space provides a valuable tool for understanding the meaning of discovered concepts within the feature space. This can lead to insights into model biases and enable safer and more reliable deployment.
    *   **Feature Space Analysis:** Investigating the composite nature of feature spaces (feature space arithmetic) allows for a deeper understanding of how models represent and combine different aspects of the input data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-written.
    *   Addresses a relevant and important problem in interpretability.
    *   The use of a conditional diffusion model is well-motivated and effective.
    *   The evaluation is comprehensive and convincing.
    *   Demonstrates potential for various applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    * The reliance on a pretrained and potentially unavailable diffusion model might limit wider adoption. While using off-the-shelf components is reasonable, mentioning availability concerns is important.
    * The computational cost of training dedicated FeatInv models per layer/architecture remains a challenge. While the paper suggests fine-tuning, it is not explored in depth.
    * The dependence on modifications of the feature space raises the possibility that manipulations are performed outside the model's scope. This could potentially lead to questionable findings with respect to the original behavior of the models.
*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of interpretability, especially for computer vision models. By providing a more powerful and flexible tool for feature space exploration, it can enable researchers to gain deeper insights into how these models work and improve their safety and reliability. It can also inspire new methods for concept steering and feature space analysis.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**
The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field of explainable AI by introducing a novel method for mapping from feature space to input space. The spatial resolution is a marked improvement over the majority of prior work. There are minor limitations regarding practical adoption and computational costs as pointed out in the "weaknesses" section, and the lack of an in-depth exploration of the method's limitations when manipulating feature space detracts from a higher score. However, the methodological soundness, comprehensive evaluation, and potential applications warrant a high score. The work represents a significant advance in visual interpretability with strong potential influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Def-DTS: Deductive Reasoning for Open-domain Dialogue Topic Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21033v1)
- **Authors**: Seungmin Lee, Yongsang Yoo, Minhwa Jung, Min Song
- **Abstract**: Dialogue Topic Segmentation (DTS) aims to divide dialogues into coherent segments. DTS plays a crucial role in various NLP downstream tasks, but suffers from chronic problems: data shortage, labeling ambiguity, and incremental complexity of recently proposed solutions. On the other hand, Despite advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) and reasoning strategies, these have rarely been applied to DTS. This paper introduces Def-DTS: Deductive Reasoning for Open-domain Dialogue Topic Segmentation, which utilizes LLM-based multi-step deductive reasoning to enhance DTS performance and enable case study using intermediate result. Our method employs a structured prompting approach for bidirectional context summarization, utterance intent classification, and deductive topic shift detection. In the intent classification process, we propose the generalizable intent list for domain-agnostic dialogue intent classification. Experiments in various dialogue settings demonstrate that Def-DTS consistently outperforms traditional and state-of-the-art approaches, with each subtask contributing to improved performance, particularly in reducing type 2 error. We also explore the potential for autolabeling, emphasizing the importance of LLM reasoning techniques in DTS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Def-DTS," a novel method for Dialogue Topic Segmentation (DTS) that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and deductive reasoning. The method uses a structured prompting approach to guide LLMs through several subtasks: bidirectional context summarization, utterance intent classification, and deductive topic shift detection. A key contribution is a generalizable intent list for domain-agnostic dialogue intent classification. Experiments across various datasets demonstrate that Def-DTS outperforms traditional and state-of-the-art approaches. The paper also explores the potential of LLM-based auto-labeling for DTS.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While leveraging LLMs for NLP tasks is not entirely new, the paper's specific application to DTS with a focus on *deductive reasoning* and a multi-step, structured prompting approach is reasonably novel. The introduction of a generalizable intent list specifically tailored for *domain-agnostic* DTS is a more substantial contribution. However, the level of innovation is incremental, building upon existing prompting and reasoning techniques.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a persistent problem in NLP: accurate and robust dialogue topic segmentation. DTS is crucial for many downstream tasks. The chronic problems of data scarcity, labeling ambiguity, and increasing model complexity are well-articulated. Def-DTS presents a viable solution that demonstrably improves performance across datasets. The exploration of auto-labeling, while preliminary, holds potential for addressing the data scarcity issue, but the observed Kappa scores indicate that the results should be regarded as tentative.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with a structured prompting approach.
    *   Empirical validation with multiple datasets and baselines.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the contribution of each subtask.
    *   Exploration of auto-labeling as a potential solution to data scarcity.
    *   The creation of a generalizable intent list for domain-agnostic dialogue is commendable.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty, while present, is incremental. The methodology is largely based on adapting existing LLM techniques to the DTS problem.
    *   The experiments heavily rely on GPT-4o. While results are presented for other LLMs, including local models, it's unclear how well Def-DTS would generalize to less capable LLMs without significant modification. The reliance on a closed-source, powerful model creates challenges for reproducibility and wider adoption. The Qwen formmating errors were not resolved, making it unclear how they would have performed.
    *   The "deductive" nature, while emphasized, is essentially a structured prompting approach that guides the LLM. The LLM still performs the reasoning. The use of deductive might mislead readers.

*   **Impact:** If widely adopted, Def-DTS could improve the performance of various NLP applications that rely on dialogue understanding. The generalizable intent list can serve as a valuable resource for future research in dialogue modeling. The results also highlight the importance of carefully designed prompts and structured reasoning for LLMs in complex NLP tasks. The potential is limited by the reliance on GPT-4o, which limits it reproducibility. The initial results on auto-labeling indicate possibilities, however, the numbers reported should be taken as initial estimates, pending further verification and refinement.

**Justification:** The paper presents a solid engineering solution to a relevant problem. Def-DTS demonstrably improves performance in DTS tasks by cleverly employing LLMs. However, it lacks profound theoretical insights or fundamental breakthroughs. The reliance on GPT-4o and modest auto-labeling results further temper the overall impact and long-term significance. The results are a result of a clever and well executed engineering approach.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLaMEA-BO: A Large Language Model Evolutionary Algorithm for Automatically Generating Bayesian Optimization Algorithms
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21034v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhu Li, Niki van Stein, Thomas Bäck, Elena Raponi
- **Abstract**: Bayesian optimization (BO) is a powerful class of algorithms for optimizing expensive black-box functions, but designing effective BO algorithms remains a manual, expertise-driven task. Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have opened new avenues for automating scientific discovery, including the automatic design of optimization algorithms. While prior work has used LLMs within optimization loops or to generate non-BO algorithms, we tackle a new challenge: Using LLMs to automatically generate full BO algorithm code. Our framework uses an evolution strategy to guide an LLM in generating Python code that preserves the key components of BO algorithms: An initial design, a surrogate model, and an acquisition function. The LLM is prompted to produce multiple candidate algorithms, which are evaluated on the established Black-Box Optimization Benchmarking (BBOB) test suite from the COmparing Continuous Optimizers (COCO) platform. Based on their performance, top candidates are selected, combined, and mutated via controlled prompt variations, enabling iterative refinement. Despite no additional fine-tuning, the LLM-generated algorithms outperform state-of-the-art BO baselines in 19 (out of 24) BBOB functions in dimension 5 and generalize well to higher dimensions, and different tasks (from the Bayesmark framework). This work demonstrates that LLMs can serve as algorithmic co-designers, offering a new paradigm for automating BO development and accelerating the discovery of novel algorithmic combinations. The source code is provided at https://github.com/Ewendawi/LLaMEA-BO.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLAMEA-BO: A Large Language Model Evolutionary Algorithm for Automatically Generating Bayesian Optimization Algorithms":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLaMEA-BO, a novel framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and an evolutionary strategy to automatically generate Bayesian Optimization (BO) algorithms.  The framework iteratively prompts an LLM to produce Python code for complete BO algorithms (including initialization, surrogate modeling, acquisition function optimization, and candidate evaluation), evaluates these algorithms on the BBOB benchmark, and refines them through crossover and mutation based on their performance.  The study demonstrates that the automatically generated BO algorithms can outperform state-of-the-art baselines on the BBOB benchmark and generalize well to higher dimensions and different tasks (Bayesmark).  The authors argue that LLaMEA-BO offers a new paradigm for automating BO development and accelerating the discovery of novel algorithmic combinations, by acting as an algorithmic co-designer.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of automated algorithm design.  While LLMs have been used for various tasks related to optimization and individual components of BO, using them to automatically generate *complete and functioning* BO algorithms is a novel approach. The extension of the LLaMEA framework to the BO domain, with BO-specific crossover and mutation operators, is a notable contribution. However, it builds directly on previous work, weakening the novelty score slightly.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to democratize and accelerate the development of optimization algorithms. BO algorithm design typically requires significant expertise.  LLaMEA-BO offers a path towards automating this process, potentially enabling researchers and practitioners without deep BO knowledge to discover new and effective algorithms. The fact that the generated algorithms can outperform existing baselines is also a key indicator of the framework's potential. The work contributes to the growing trend of using LLMs for scientific discovery and automated machine learning. Furthermore, the provided analysis and ablations contribute to understanding the capabilities and limitations of this automated approach to BO.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework's design is sound, combining the generative power of LLMs with the exploratory strength of an evolutionary algorithm.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive. The authors benchmark the generated algorithms extensively on BBOB, Bayesmark, and other synthetic tasks, across multiple dimensions and instances.
    *   The paper provides valuable insights into the design choices and hyperparameter settings of the LLaMEA-BO framework through the conducted ablation studies.
    *   The open-sourcing of code and experimental data is commendable and facilitates reproducibility and future research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study relies on a single LLM backend (Gemini 2.0 flash). While this is understandable due to resource constraints, exploring other LLMs and their impact on the quality of generated algorithms would strengthen the findings.
    *   The empirical analysis is limited to noiseless, continuous, single-objective optimization problems. This limits the broader applicability of the demonstrated approach to other types of optimization problems such as mixed-integer, constrained, noisy, or multi-objective problems, which are extremely common in real-world applications.
    *   The prominence of trust-region variants in the high-performing discoveries might indicate a bias stemming from the initial BO template used to guide the LLM. Further investigation and experimentation with different templates and BO components is warranted.
    *   The limitations section acknowledges that memorization could be a contributing factor and may skew some results, although the diversity of tasks, generalization to more difficult problems, and open-source release should mitigate this. More effort could be done to actively eliminate memorization through various sampling strategies, although this is a common challenge for LLM-based research.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of automated algorithm design. LLaMEA-BO provides a promising new direction for automating the development of BO algorithms. While the study does have limitations, particularly regarding the scope of evaluated problem settings and potential biases in the algorithm generation process, its strengths – the novelty of the approach, comprehensive experimental validation, and open-source release of resources – make it a worthwhile contribution. The ability to produce working and outperforming BO algorithms makes this more than a simple LLM experiment. For these reasons, I assess a significance score higher than other similar LLM-driven works.

### RainFusion: Adaptive Video Generation Acceleration via Multi-Dimensional Visual Redundancy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21036v1)
- **Authors**: Aiyue Chen, Bin Dong, Jingru Li, Jing Lin, Yiwu Yao, Gongyi Wang
- **Abstract**: Video generation using diffusion models is highly computationally intensive, with 3D attention in Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models accounting for over 80\% of the total computational resources. In this work, we introduce {\bf RainFusion}, a novel training-free sparse attention method that exploits inherent sparsity nature in visual data to accelerate attention computation while preserving video quality. Specifically, we identify three unique sparse patterns in video generation attention calculations--Spatial Pattern, Temporal Pattern and Textural Pattern. The sparse pattern for each attention head is determined online with negligible overhead (\textasciitilde\,0.2\%) with our proposed {\bf ARM} (Adaptive Recognition Module) during inference. Our proposed {\bf RainFusion} is a plug-and-play method, that can be seamlessly integrated into state-of-the-art 3D-attention video generation models without additional training or calibration. We evaluate our method on leading open-sourced models including HunyuanVideo, OpenSoraPlan-1.2 and CogVideoX-5B, demonstrating its broad applicability and effectiveness. Experimental results show that RainFusion achieves over {\bf 2\(\times\)} speedup in attention computation while maintaining video quality, with only a minimal impact on VBench scores (-0.2\%).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the RainFusion paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RainFusion: Adaptive Video Generation Acceleration via Multi-Dimensional Visual Redundancy" introduces a novel training-free sparse attention method to accelerate video generation using diffusion models. The approach, dubbed RainFusion, leverages inherent sparsity patterns in visual data by identifying three unique types of sparsity: Spatial, Temporal, and Textural. An Adaptive Recognition Module (ARM) dynamically determines the sparse pattern for each attention head during inference with minimal overhead.  The method is designed to be plug-and-play, integrating seamlessly into existing 3D-attention video generation models without retraining or calibration. Experiments on models like HunyuanVideo, OpenSoraPlan-1.2, and CogVideoX-5B demonstrate over 2x speedup in attention computation while maintaining video quality with a small VBench score impact.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in the identification and exploitation of distinct spatial, temporal, and textural sparsity patterns *specifically* within the context of video generation using 3D-attention diffusion models. While sparse attention mechanisms exist in other areas of deep learning, the targeted application and the explicit characterization of these three sparsity types are unique. The ARM module, although relatively simple, provides a practical way to dynamically adapt to these patterns. The plug-and-play nature is also a strength from a usability perspective.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a key bottleneck in video generation: the high computational cost of 3D attention. By achieving a ~2x speedup while maintaining quality, RainFusion makes these models more accessible and practical. Its training-free nature is particularly appealing because it avoids the substantial cost associated with retraining models. Furthermore, it can be used in conjunction with other speed-up techniques.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the computational bottleneck in video diffusion models.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** RainFusion is presented as a well-defined, modular approach.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** Evaluation across multiple state-of-the-art models strengthens the method's claim of general applicability.
    *   **Quantifiable Results:** The reported speedup and VBench scores provide concrete evidence of the method's effectiveness and minimal quality degradation.
    *   **Training-Free:** Avoids the high costs of retraining.
    *   **Modularity:** Can be combined with other acceleration techniques (like A-DiT) to further improve performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited ARM Complexity:** The ARM module, while practical, is conceptually relatively simple. The approach primarily relies on identifying existing patterns but doesn't introduce sophisticated methods for creating or learning sparsity patterns.
    *   **VBench Score Impact:** Although the impact is described as "minimal" (~ -0.2%), even small decreases in VBench scores can matter to users. A more in-depth analysis of specific dimensions within VBench that are affected would be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:**  The paper relies heavily on empirical results. A more theoretical explanation of why these specific sparsity patterns are effective could further strengthen the contribution.
    *   **Dependency on existing attention architectures:** while the module provides improvement to existing architecture, the underlying attention module itself is computationally heavy.

*   **Potential Influence:** RainFusion has the potential to influence future research by:
    *   Encouraging further exploration of sparsity in video generation.
    *   Inspiring more sophisticated adaptive sparsity techniques.
    *   Providing a practical building block for faster video generation pipelines.

**Overall Score:**

Given the identified strengths and weaknesses, along with its potential impact on the field, the score is 7. This paper presents a clearly articulated and practically useful solution to a relevant problem with compelling empirical results. It lacks a high level of theoretical depth or architectural complexity but provides valuable engineering insight and immediate usability.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### FCKT: Fine-Grained Cross-Task Knowledge Transfer with Semantic Contrastive Learning for Targeted Sentiment Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21040v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Chen, Zhao Zhang, Meng Yuan, Kepeng Xu, Fuzhen Zhuang
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we address the task of targeted sentiment analysis (TSA), which involves two sub-tasks, i.e., identifying specific aspects from reviews and determining their corresponding sentiments. Aspect extraction forms the foundation for sentiment prediction, highlighting the critical dependency between these two tasks for effective cross-task knowledge transfer. While most existing studies adopt a multi-task learning paradigm to align task-specific features in the latent space, they predominantly rely on coarse-grained knowledge transfer. Such approaches lack fine-grained control over aspect-sentiment relationships, often assuming uniform sentiment polarity within related aspects. This oversimplification neglects contextual cues that differentiate sentiments, leading to negative transfer. To overcome these limitations, we propose FCKT, a fine-grained cross-task knowledge transfer framework tailored for TSA. By explicitly incorporating aspect-level information into sentiment prediction, FCKT achieves fine-grained knowledge transfer, effectively mitigating negative transfer and enhancing task performance. Experiments on three datasets, including comparisons with various baselines and large language models (LLMs), demonstrate the effectiveness of FCKT. The source code is available on https://github.com/cwei01/FCKT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FCKT: Fine-Grained Cross-Task Knowledge Transfer with Semantic Contrastive Learning for Targeted Sentiment Analysis":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FCKT, a novel framework for targeted sentiment analysis (TSA). FCKT addresses the limitations of existing approaches that rely on coarse-grained knowledge transfer between aspect extraction and sentiment prediction. The authors argue that coarse-grained methods often assume uniform sentiment polarity within related aspects, leading to negative transfer when contextual cues indicate otherwise. FCKT incorporates fine-grained aspect-level information into sentiment prediction using a token-level semantic contrastive learning mechanism. This mechanism refines the model's understanding of aspects by treating start and end tokens of the same aspect as positive pairs and unrelated tokens as negative pairs.  To address insufficient supervision for sentiment classification, an alternating learning strategy is employed, integrating real labeled data with predicted transfer knowledge. Experimental results on three datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of FCKT compared to baselines, including large language models (LLMs).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in the specific combination of techniques to achieve fine-grained cross-task knowledge transfer in TSA. The token-level semantic contrastive learning and the alternating learning strategy, specifically designed for aspect-sentiment relationships, appear to be a novel combination. While contrastive learning has been used in ABSA before, the token-level application for aspect extraction and the integration with alternating learning are unique.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is in improving the accuracy and robustness of TSA, a crucial task in natural language processing with applications in customer feedback analysis and other domains. By addressing the issue of negative transfer associated with coarse-grained knowledge transfer, FCKT offers a way to build more reliable sentiment analysis systems. The comparison with LLMs also highlights the continuing relevance of task-specific models, especially in resource-constrained scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing coarse-grained approaches and motivates the need for a fine-grained approach.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The FCKT framework is clearly explained, with details on the token-level contrastive learning and alternating learning strategy.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate that FCKT outperforms various baselines, including state-of-the-art models and LLMs, across multiple datasets.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of individual components of the FCKT framework.
    *   **Case Study and Error Analysis:** The qualitative analysis (case study) and the error analysis give an insight into the model's performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Heuristic Extraction Method:** The authors acknowledge that the heuristic aspect extraction method might filter out some false negatives, leading to lower recall. A more sophisticated aspect extraction method could potentially improve overall performance.
    *   **Dataset Diversity Limitations:** While the paper uses three datasets, more diversity in the datasets could further validate the generalizability of the proposed framework.
    *   **Computational Complexity:** The time complexity is O(n^2+nh), which is somewhat high if using long sentences.
    *   **Writing Quality:** There are occasional grammatical errors/typos throughout the paper (e.g., on Page 5, the phrase "improving task perfor-mance. Noted TSA remains a challenging task", or, on Page 3, "A depiction of the peopose FCKT framework.") that could be resolved with a thorough editorial review.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in TSA by providing a more effective approach to cross-task knowledge transfer. The proposed techniques could be adapted to other related tasks in natural language processing where fine-grained dependencies between sub-tasks are crucial.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-validated approach to TSA, addressing a clear limitation of existing methods.  The specific combination of token-level contrastive learning and the alternating training strategy, along with the empirical results demonstrating superior performance, justify a relatively high score.  However, the reliance on a heuristic aspect extraction method and the limited dataset diversity prevent a score in the very top range.

### Large Language Model-enhanced Reinforcement Learning for Low-Altitude Economy Networking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21045v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyi Cai, Ruichen Zhang, Changyuan Zhao, Yu Zhang, Jiawen Kang, Dusit Niyato, Tao Jiang, Xuemin Shen
- **Abstract**: Low-Altitude Economic Networking (LAENet) aims to support diverse flying applications below 1,000 meters by deploying various aerial vehicles for flexible and cost-effective aerial networking. However, complex decision-making, resource constraints, and environmental uncertainty pose significant challenges to the development of the LAENet. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers a potential solution in response to these challenges but has limitations in generalization, reward design, and model stability. The emergence of large language models (LLMs) offers new opportunities for RL to mitigate these limitations. In this paper, we first present a tutorial about integrating LLMs into RL by using the capacities of generation, contextual understanding, and structured reasoning of LLMs. We then propose an LLM-enhanced RL framework for the LAENet in terms of serving the LLM as information processor, reward designer, decision-maker, and generator. Moreover, we conduct a case study by using LLMs to design a reward function to improve the learning performance of RL in the LAENet. Finally, we provide a conclusion and discuss future work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Large Language Model-enhanced Reinforcement Learning for Low-Altitude Economy Networking" proposes a framework that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with Reinforcement Learning (RL) to address challenges in Low-Altitude Economic Networking (LAENet).  The LAENet involves managing aerial vehicles (UAVs) for various applications, but faces complexities due to real-time decision-making, environmental uncertainty, and resource constraints. The paper argues that while RL offers solutions, its limitations in generalization, reward design, and model stability can be mitigated by incorporating LLMs. The proposed framework utilizes LLMs as information processors, reward designers, decision-makers, and simulators.  The paper also includes a case study demonstrating how LLMs can be used to design reward functions to improve energy consumption optimization in a UAV-assisted IoT scenario within the LAENet.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of LLM-enhanced RL to the specific domain of LAENet. While LLM-enhanced RL is a burgeoning area, its application to low-altitude networking, with its specific challenges of UAV control, dynamic environments, and resource constraints, offers a unique contribution. The paper synthesizes existing LLM-RL methods but adapts them for the nuances of LAENet. The proposition of using LLMs in multiple roles (information processor, reward designer, etc.) in the framework offers incremental, albeit well-defined, novelty.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work could be significant. LAENet is a developing field with numerous practical applications (transportation, disaster response, etc.).  By addressing the limitations of traditional RL in this context, the proposed LLM-enhanced RL framework can contribute to more efficient, robust, and adaptable UAV control systems. The paper showcases how LLMs can automate the otherwise tedious process of reward function engineering and potentially lead to better policies. The case study, although limited, illustrates the potential for improved energy optimization.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive overview of how LLMs address limitations of classical RL.
    *   Well-defined LLM-enhanced RL framework specifically tailored for LAENet.
    *   Clear articulation of LLM's multiple roles within the RL loop.
    *   Case study demonstrating the practical benefits of LLM-designed reward functions.
    *   Thorough background review and discussion of related work.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The case study is limited in scope and scale. It demonstrates the benefit of LLM-designed reward functions in a specific scenario, but it lacks extensive experimentation and comparison with other state-of-the-art methods.
    *   While the paper outlines the potential for LLMs as information processors, decision-makers, and simulators, these aspects are not fully explored in the case study or through concrete examples.
    *   The paper does not deeply address the computational costs and latency associated with integrating LLMs into real-time UAV control systems. The practical feasibility of deploying LLMs on resource-constrained aerial vehicles needs further examination.
    *   The risk of LLM hallucinations (generating nonsensical information) and biases when applied to reward design are not fully mitigated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research in several ways:

    *   Encourage exploration of LLM-enhanced RL for other aspects of LAENet, such as UAV fleet management, dynamic resource allocation, and collision avoidance.
    *   Stimulate research on more efficient and robust methods for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments.
    *   Inspire the development of more sophisticated techniques for mitigating LLM hallucinations and biases in RL reward design.

**Justification for Score:**
The paper presents a reasonably novel and potentially significant contribution by applying LLM-enhanced RL to the emerging field of LAENet. It provides a comprehensive framework and a preliminary case study that showcase the benefits of this approach. However, the limitations of the case study, the lack of exploration into the other LLM roles, and the unaddressed computational costs warrant a cautious assessment. While the paper’s framework is novel and well-articulated, the evidence backing its superiority is limited to a single case study of reasonable complexity. The case study is convincing but not conclusive. The study shows that LLM-assisted rewards can improve performance compared to hand-crafted rewards, yet it doesn't address the computational overhead associated with the process.

### SHE-LoRA: Selective Homomorphic Encryption for Federated Tuning with Heterogeneous LoRA
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21051v1)
- **Authors**: Jianmin Liu, Li Yan, Borui Li, Lei Yu, Chao Shen
- **Abstract**: Federated fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs) is critical for improving their performance in handling domain-specific tasks. However, prior work has shown that clients' private data can actually be recovered via gradient inversion attacks. Existing privacy preservation techniques against such attacks typically entail performance degradation and high costs, making them ill-suited for clients with heterogeneous data distributions and device capabilities. In this paper, we propose SHE-LoRA, which integrates selective homomorphic encryption (HE) and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) to enable efficient and privacy-preserving federated tuning of LLMs in cross-device environment. Heterogeneous clients adaptively select partial model parameters for homomorphic encryption based on parameter sensitivity assessment, with the encryption subset obtained via negotiation. To ensure accurate model aggregation, we design a column-aware secure aggregation method and customized reparameterization techniques to align the aggregation results with the heterogeneous device capabilities of clients. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SHE-LoRA maintains performance comparable to non-private baselines, achieves strong resistance to the state-of-the-art attacks, and significantly reduces communication overhead by 94.901\% and encryption computation overhead by 99.829\%, compared to baseline. Our code is accessible at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/SHE-LoRA-8D84.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SHE-LORA: Selective Homomorphic Encryption for Federated Tuning with Heterogeneous LoRA" addresses the problem of privacy leakage in federated fine-tuning (PEFT) of large language models (LLMs) using LoRA. It proposes a method called SHE-LORA, which combines selective homomorphic encryption (SHE) and LoRA to enable efficient and privacy-preserving federated tuning, particularly in cross-device environments with heterogeneous clients. The key ideas include: 1) Clients selectively encrypt only sensitive model parameters based on local resource constraints and a negotiated global encryption subset. 2) A column-aware secure aggregation scheme is used to handle the encrypted data efficiently. 3) Customized reparameterization techniques are employed to align the aggregation results with the heterogeneous device capabilities of the clients. Experiments on OpenLLaMA-3B and BERT-Large demonstrate that SHE-LORA maintains comparable performance to non-private baselines, resists state-of-the-art attacks, and significantly reduces communication and encryption overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a valuable integration of existing techniques (SHE and LoRA) with novel modifications tailored for the federated learning setting.  The key novelty lies in *adaptively* selecting which parameters to encrypt based on both sensitivity analysis *and* client-specific constraints, and combining this with the column-aware aggregation and reparameterization.  This adaptive selection addresses a significant limitation of previous SHE-based approaches that often rely on fixed encryption strategies, making them less efficient and practical for heterogeneous systems. The column-swapping obfuscation method, though relatively simple, adds another layer of security.

*   **Significance:**  The paper has the potential to be significant because it offers a practical solution to a critical problem: privacy-preserving federated learning of LLMs. The experimental results, particularly the large reductions in communication and encryption overhead, are compelling. By allowing for client heterogeneity and adapting encryption to resource constraints, SHE-LORA makes federated LLM tuning more accessible to real-world scenarios where devices have varying capabilities. The resistance to gradient inversion attacks, which are known threats to FL, further enhances the method's practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Adaptive Encryption:** The client-specific encryption budget and server-side negotiation are strong points, enabling the system to balance privacy and efficiency in heterogeneous environments.
    *   **Efficient Aggregation:** The column-aware aggregation reduces the computational overhead associated with HE.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed, with comparisons to relevant baselines and an analysis of resistance to privacy attacks.  The experiments cover diverse LLMs and tasks.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and addresses the challenges of applying SHE to federated LoRA tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Trust Model:**  The reliance on an "honest-but-curious" server is a common assumption in the HE literature, but it is a limitation.  The paper acknowledges this and suggests potential future work to relax this assumption (multi-key HE), but that is not part of the current contribution.  A more in-depth discussion of alternative trust models could strengthen the paper.
    *   **Encryption Subset Negotiation Complexity:** The complexity and potential overhead of the encryption subset negotiation process are not fully explored. While the paper claims HE efficiency, the communication needed for negotiation itself is worth considering.
    *   **Limited Attack Analysis:** The paper demonstrates resistance to DAGER, but more extensive testing against other state-of-the-art inversion attacks would be beneficial.
    *   **OPE discussion and security**: Order Preserving Encryption (OPE) has been known to have limited security in practice, and the work uses OPE in its SHE-LORA protocol. This weakness could be exploited in a different type of security attack.

*   **Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in privacy-preserving federated learning.  The adaptive encryption strategy and the system-level optimizations for HE are valuable contributions that can be built upon. Other researchers may focus on enhancing the security of the proposed SHE-LORA by using different types of OPE, using multi-key HE, or designing more robust protection against gradient inversion attacks.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by addressing a practical problem with a well-engineered solution. The adaptive encryption scheme, combined with optimizations for the FL setting, is innovative and significant. The experimental results are compelling and support the claims. While the trust model and some security and scalability aspects are not fully explored, the paper provides a strong foundation for future research. The OPE usage should be explicitly justified with more robust security analysis in the paper. Therefore, the score is a 7.5 out of 10.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Study of Lightweight Transformer Architectures for Single-Channel Speech Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21057v1)
- **Authors**: Haixin Zhao, Nilesh Madhu
- **Abstract**: In speech enhancement, achieving state-of-the-art (SotA) performance while adhering to the computational constraints on edge devices remains a formidable challenge. Networks integrating stacked temporal and spectral modelling effectively leverage improved architectures such as transformers; however, they inevitably incur substantial computational complexity and model expansion. Through systematic ablation analysis on transformer-based temporal and spectral modelling, we demonstrate that the architecture employing streamlined Frequency-Time-Frequency (FTF) stacked transformers efficiently learns global dependencies within causal context, while avoiding considerable computational demands. Utilising discriminators in training further improves learning efficacy and enhancement without introducing additional complexity during inference. The proposed lightweight, causal, transformer-based architecture with adversarial training (LCT-GAN) yields SoTA performance on instrumental metrics among contemporary lightweight models, but with far less overhead. Compared to DeepFilterNet2, the LCT-GAN only requires 6% of the parameters, at similar complexity and performance. Against CCFNet+(Lite), LCT-GAN saves 9% in parameters and 10% in multiply-accumulate operations yet yielding improved performance. Further, the LCT-GAN even outperforms more complex, common baseline models on widely used test datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a lightweight, causal, transformer-based Generative Adversarial Network (GAN) architecture (LCT-GAN) for single-channel speech enhancement, specifically designed for resource-constrained environments. The core innovation lies in a streamlined Frequency-Time-Frequency (FTF) transformer block.  This block aims to efficiently capture global dependencies in speech signals while minimizing computational overhead.  The paper demonstrates through ablation studies the effectiveness of the FTF structure and real-valued mask estimation. The LCT-GAN also uses multi-scale and multi-period discriminators to enhance speech component preservation during training.  The paper reports state-of-the-art (SotA) performance among lightweight models, achieving comparable or better results than existing models like DeepFilterNet with significantly fewer parameters and MACs. The authors further explore the use of perceptual contrast stretching (PCS) to improve intrusive metrics.  The paper includes evaluations on the Voicebank+Demand and DNS3 datasets, and audio samples are provided online.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Significant Parameter Reduction:** The primary strength of the paper lies in its focus on creating a truly lightweight model for speech enhancement. Achieving comparable or better performance than DeepFilterNet with a fraction of the parameters (≈6%) is a notable accomplishment.
    * **FTF Transformer Design:** The proposed FTF transformer structure is a clever way to exploit global dependencies while managing computational complexity. The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the efficacy of this design choice. The use of parameter sharing within the GRU module in the time transformer is another valuable optimization.
    * **Causality:** The emphasis on causality is important for real-time applications.
    * **GAN-based Training:**  The use of GAN training, especially with multi-scale and multi-period discriminators, demonstrably improves speech quality metrics.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough evaluations on standard datasets, including real-recorded data from the DNS3 challenge, which adds to the credibility of the results. The ablation study on complex-valued outputs is also helpful.
    * **Reproducibility:** The provision of audio samples enhances reproducibility and allows for subjective assessment.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Novelty in GAN Components:** While the FTF transformer is novel, the use of GANs and multi-scale discriminators is relatively standard in speech enhancement and audio processing.  The improvements stemming from these components, while beneficial, are incremental.
    * **Over-Suppression Artifacts:** The paper acknowledges some over-suppression of fricatives and sibilants. This limitation needs further investigation and mitigation strategies.
    * **PCS Dependence (Voicebank+Demand):** The fact that LCT-GAN's PESQ score on Voicebank+Demand is lower than DeepFilterNet3 without PCS raises a concern.  Relying on PCS as a post-processing step adds complexity and might mask underlying limitations of the core architecture. The SI-SDR metric being not applicable during PCS also raise question on the evaluation.
    * **Presentation Clarity:** While generally well-written, some sections (e.g., the description of the loss function weights) could be more clearly explained.

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty resides in the FTF transformer architecture and its application within a lightweight, causal speech enhancement framework. The clever engineering to reduce parameters and MACs is a significant contribution. While GAN-based training and discriminators are not new, their specific application in conjunction with the FTF block and with a focus on minimal computational overhead adds to the novelty.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in making SotA speech enhancement performance more accessible to resource-constrained devices. The reduced parameter count and computational complexity open up possibilities for deployment on edge devices and real-time applications. This has implications for various applications, including mobile devices, hearing aids, and IoT devices.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of speech enhancement. The LCT-GAN model achieves a good balance between performance and computational efficiency. The FTF transformer is a novel and well-justified design choice. However, some weaknesses prevent it from achieving a higher score.  The dependence on PCS for certain metrics on Voicebank+Demand and the presence of over-suppression artifacts suggest areas for further improvement. Also, the GAN training components offer less architectural novelty compared to the FTF transformer. While it is a good work for constrained environments it should be noted that the performance may not be at par with the heavy models out there.

### Disentangling Locality and Entropy in Ranking Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21058v1)
- **Authors**: Andrew Parry, Debasis Ganguly, Sean MacAvaney
- **Abstract**: The training process of ranking models involves two key data selection decisions: a sampling strategy, and a labeling strategy. Modern ranking systems, especially those for performing semantic search, typically use a ``hard negative'' sampling strategy to identify challenging items using heuristics and a distillation labeling strategy to transfer ranking "knowledge" from a more capable model. In practice, these approaches have grown increasingly expensive and complex, for instance, popular pretrained rankers from SentenceTransformers involve 12 models in an ensemble with data provenance hampering reproducibility. Despite their complexity, modern sampling and labeling strategies have not been fully ablated, leaving the underlying source of effectiveness gains unclear. Thus, to better understand why models improve and potentially reduce the expense of training effective models, we conduct a broad ablation of sampling and distillation processes in neural ranking. We frame and theoretically derive the orthogonal nature of model geometry affected by example selection and the effect of teacher ranking entropy on ranking model optimization, establishing conditions in which data augmentation can effectively improve bias in a ranking model. Empirically, our investigation on established benchmarks and common architectures shows that sampling processes that were once highly effective in contrastive objectives may be spurious or harmful under distillation. We further investigate how data augmentation, in terms of inputs and targets, can affect effectiveness and the intrinsic behavior of models in ranking. Through this work, we aim to encourage more computationally efficient approaches that reduce focus on contrastive pairs and instead directly understand training dynamics under rankings, which better represent real-world settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the interplay between sampling strategies and distillation techniques in neural ranking. It argues that modern ranking systems rely on complex and expensive pipelines involving hard negative mining and distillation, often without clear understanding of the individual contributions. The authors theoretically derive a generalization bound for ranking distillation relating model geometry and teacher ranking entropy, showing that sample locality and teacher entropy influence generalization.  Empirically, they ablate common training settings controlling for example locality and target entropy, demonstrating that complex pipelines can be comparable to naive approaches under semi-supervision and provide evidence for factors influencing model effectiveness. The work aims to encourage computationally efficient approaches that directly understand training dynamics under rankings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution lies in the theoretical framing and empirical ablation of common practices in neural ranking. Deriving a generalization bound specifically for ranking distillation that considers sample locality and teacher entropy is novel. While individual techniques like hard negative mining and distillation are well-established, analyzing their *interaction* within a ranking context, and providing theoretical justification for observed behaviors, represents a valuable contribution. The explicit formulation of how model geometry affected by sampling and the teacher entropy affects optimization is strong. The empirical results showing that simpler approaches can be comparable to complex pipelines are also novel and challenge common assumptions.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significant for several reasons:

    *   **Efficiency:** The work highlights that simpler and computationally cheaper approaches can be competitive with complex pipelines. This has practical implications for reducing training and inference costs in ranking systems.
    *   **Understanding:** By providing theoretical and empirical insights, the paper contributes to a deeper understanding of the factors that truly drive performance in neural ranking. It challenges the uncritical adoption of increasingly complex data augmentation pipelines.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The focus on ablations and isolating effects addresses a growing concern about reproducibility in deep learning research, especially in areas like ranking where pipelines are often complex and poorly documented.
    *   **Generalizability:** The generalization bound offers insights into how well a model trained with distillation will generalize to new data.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical grounding:** The formalization of the problem and the derivation of the generalization bound are strong aspects of the paper.
    *   **Comprehensive ablations:** The thorough empirical investigation across different datasets, models, and loss functions provides convincing evidence for the claims.
    *   **Clear writing:** While dealing with complex concepts, the paper is well-written and structured, making it relatively accessible.
    *   The theoretical analysis uses a combination of VC dimension and Lipschitz continuity, which is strong.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Assumptions:** The theoretical analysis makes certain assumptions (e.g., Lipschitz continuity, Bregman divergences) that may not always hold in practice. The impact of these assumptions on the conclusions could be discussed further.
    *   **Limited scope of datasets:** The paper focused on particular families of datasets (BEIR, TREC Deep Learning). Expanding the analysis to include broader range of datasets, especially in industrial settings, will strengthens the conclusions.
    *   **Lack of a "better" method:**  While the paper demonstrates the limitations of complex methods, it does not propose a specific, clearly superior alternative. The focus is on understanding rather than proposing a concrete solution. More guidance as to what direction to go would significantly increase value.
    *   Density-ratio adjustment can be improved with recent data-driven approaches. This reduces significance.
    *   The score distribution analysis, while interesting, could be better connected to actionable outcomes.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence researchers in information retrieval and NLP to be more critical and systematic in their development of ranking systems. It provides a framework for understanding the effects of different training techniques and encourages a focus on efficiency and reproducibility. The generalization bound could be used as a tool for guiding the design of ranking systems.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning this paper a score of 7.  While the paper's individual components (distillation, negative sampling, etc.) aren't groundbreaking, the **novel synthesis of these components within a rigorous theoretical framework and the subsequent comprehensive ablation study are significant**. The paper effectively challenges common assumptions about the necessity of complex ranking pipelines, offers practical implications for improving efficiency, and contributes to a deeper understanding of the factors that drive performance. The limitations are that the paper doesn't provide a concrete, better approach and makes certain assumptions that might not hold in all practical settings. The impact on the field will likely be to shift research towards more principled and efficient methods for neural ranking and a healthy dose of skepticism toward unvalidated complexity. The combination of theoretical analysis and large-scale experimentation gives the paper a relatively high degree of influence, while providing value to current researchers.
